## Researcher Response 9

**For the variable coherence rule, the category of arities with free temporal composition required in Q1(a) exists for every admissible $E$.** Its strict labels are the $E$-values actually realized by raw terms on their positional arities. They form a globular operad $J$, and raw syntax is a cellular resolution of $J$ with lifting at every prescribed globular boundary. This addresses both the inner directed exchanges and the choice of a witness in the arity step of round-1 Response 222. The separate comparison with models on an arbitrary computad is specified below.

### The operad of realizable directed cells

Write $H Z$ for raw syntax on a globular set of generators, $F Z$ for its ordinary free strict category and $\pi$ for ordinary projection. For $d\in(FZ)_n$ let $P_d$ be its positional tree and $b_d$ its total elevated to rank $n$. Define
$$
 (JZ)_d =
 \{\,\llbracket t\rrbracket_E \mid t\in (H P_d)_n,\quad
                  \pi\llbracket t\rrbracket_E=b_d\,\}.
$$
Retain the index $d$ in forming $JZ$. For a tree $P$, this identifies $JP$ with the degreewise raw image in $F_E(P)$. Indeed the supporting subtree of $d\in FP$ includes in $P$ with a raw clamping retraction. Every representative strengthens to this subtree by typed support. The strict inclusion is split injective, so equality there is exactly equality after inclusion. For general $Z$ the definition compares values **before** folding the positions onto the generators of $Z$.

Here are the globular and substitution checks. Send a raw term to the $E$-value of its unique raw lift at $b_d$. Two representatives with the same entry have equal $E$-faces on the positional tree. Restricting a signed face to its own support by clamping therefore gives equal face entries, including when both signed supports are the same. Relabelling globular generators changes only the labels of the positions in $d$. Consequently the resulting map $J\to F$ is cartesian: given a relabelling and a lift of the ordinary cell, the same entry at the unchanged positional total is its unique lift in $J$.

The map $H Z\to JZ$ lifts every cell at prescribed parallel raw faces. Lift the faces at the two signed sections of $b_d$ by the raw positional bijection. Their common lower boundary lifts literally to the same terms. Their $E$-values are the faces of the specified entry by the split support inclusions. Choose any raw representative of the entry on $P_d$ and fit it to these raw faces by the identity-image comparisons of Response 3. Relabel to $Z$. Its entry is still the one specified, and raw uniqueness returns the two original faces. Objects lift as vertices. Each calculation is finite, so the statement also holds for infinite globular sets. [Support and boundary fitting](#researcher-response-3) · [Positional bijections](#researcher-response-5)

In particular a finite compatible tuple of entries has a simultaneous raw representative. Lift its generating positions in order, prescribing the raw faces already chosen. To substitute a tuple into an entry, substitute such representatives into a representative of the operation. Independence of choices can be tested on the positional tree of the composite. There ordinary strict multiplication splits the specified total into its successive input subtrees. Each input has the $E$-value of its entry included from the corresponding subtree, by uniqueness of positional lifting. Applying the outer $E$-cell to this strict tuple computes the entry of the result by requirement 5. Thus all choices give the same result. For a twice subdivided total the two splits agree by cartesianity and associativity of ordinary strict multiplication. The resulting $E$-substitutions compose, giving associativity in $J$. The one-variable tuple gives the unit. This makes $H\to J\to F$ maps of monads. The cartesian map $J\to F$ makes $J$ a globular operad: pullbacks for its functor, unit and multiplication follow by pulling back those for $F$. Explicitly, for multiplication first lift the ordinary subdivided cell, then restore each entry at its lifted positions. The entry of the flattened result is forced by cartesianity over its ordinary value. [Free strict multiplication and globular operads, Chapter 8 and Appendix F](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0305049)

The resolution assertion here has an algebraic meaning. Raw syntax adjoins one generator for each admitted head at its tree and its written generic boundary. The boundary expressions and their heads precede this generator in the finite formation derivation. There are no raw equations between independent heads. Hence a map from $H$ to a globular operad lifts through any operad map with the globe-boundary lifting property on collections. Assign the head a lift of its specified image at the already assigned boundary and extend to applications by substitution. At the generic total this is exactly a lifting problem in its collection fibre. Conversely the preceding prescribed-face proof supplies these lifts for $H\to J$ itself. This proves cellular cofibrancy and the boundary lifting assertion over $J$ without a model-structure assumption about $H$-algebras.

### The arity category and its two comparisons

Apply the normal-expression construction of round-1 Response 222 with strict tags in $J(P)$. For clarity, $M(Q)$ has variables, canonical identities, flattened lists of nonidentity top factors, and atoms on non-temporal arities. An atom specifies a full ordinary-support tag in $J(P)$, parallel generic $M(P)$-faces at its strict faces, and a compatible labelling by $M(Q)$-cells. Erase identity-labelled tips before forming an atom, transporting its generic faces and its **whole tag** by the pruning substitution. At a temporal residual use its temporal word. On the other residuals use the identity when the tag is an identity and its generic faces coincide. Otherwise retain the atom. Arrows $P\to Q$ of the arity category are the globular tuples in $M(Q)$ indexed by $P$.

The following verifications permit this construction with directed interchange at every cut.

1. A tag has a raw representative. Pruning applies the strict substitution to that representative, giving a realizable tag. Its ordinary support is the full residual arity by the support union for substitutions. Two available deletions commute on the entire original tuple. Therefore they commute on the tag and, by the lower-rank induction, on the formal faces. Substituting another tuple replays the previous deletions before erasing newly trivial tips. The resulting expressions agree, with concatenation on lists. This proves functoriality even for a strict tag whose Gray projection is an identity.
2. On a temporal row $J=F$. Such a row has no fork, so Response 7 gives the strict comparison and raw realization at the total of every subinterval. A normal expression on a temporal target is a positional cell, an interval word, or an iterated identity. Indeed, once its proper labels are in this form, a non-temporal atom there would give an ordinary tree map to a row with no identity at a maximal position. After pruning identity tips, a map into a row has temporal source by the strict-tree factorization of Response 222. This excludes the atom. The temporal evaluation has the required faces since its lower-dimensional expressions already have the indicated interval values.
3. In an arbitrary target the category in each top rank is free on the variables and retained atoms at that rank. Strict $E$-relations between composites are evaluated in $J$; the temporal words in $M$ have just concatenation and the empty word. The map $M(P)\to JP$ lifts at prescribed parallel formal faces. Restrict them to the supporting subtree of the proposed tag. This is possible because variables used in a normal expression lie in its ordinary support, by fullness for atoms and support union for lists. The restricted tag belongs to $J$ by its raw representative and the split inclusion. Use the constructor there at the restricted faces and then include back. Each constructor case has the indicated value and faces. At identity-tag equal faces use their formal identity, and on a temporal residual use item 2.
4. Interpreting the raw heads by these constructors gives maps of raw algebras $H P\to M(P)\to JP$ with composite the original entry map. A head formed at an interchange-free converted generic type has a realizable tag too. Elaborate its generic faces and identity tuple on its own tree by Response 3. This preserves its strict boundary and the prescribed choice at that boundary. It is the **chosen cell** which is substituted later, so there is no demand for a shortest-path choice to commute with pruning. For the word-coherence equation the coherence-case choice is the identity on its common strict face. Additional $E$-endomorphisms there do not change that choice.
5. The protecting sections for simultaneous pruning exist at every cut. The leaf removed at cut $k$ has degree exactly $k+1$. A section entry in the adjacent column composes that leaf with a single disc at cut $k$; each face is the corresponding shorter whisker. These arities and their prunings have no fork. They thus have the ordinary strict tags and admit the required raw heads by Response 7. Under the projection back, the leaf becomes an identity, and the normal constructor on the residual disc returns its variable. Choosing which endpoint to keep protects the other designated peak as in round-1 Response 183. The very same single-disc and temporal arities account for every directed binary word constructor and its formal faces. Thus the word-core reduction of round-1 Response 170 uses only arities on which the required unit computations hold.

It follows in particular that $H P\to M(P)$ becomes an equivalence in any localization of raw algebras inverting maps with prescribed-boundary lifting. Both $M(P)\to JP$ and the composite have that lifting property. This comparison on trees and the free category in item 3 are the two different ingredients needed by the temporal-site argument. For the remaining arbitrary-context step of Q1(a), the use of a raw homotopy theory can now be isolated. A sufficient additional input is the raw derived-nerve and boundary-comparison theorem used in round-1 Responses 50 and 222, for these variable-form raw algebras. More explicitly, for each free raw $Y=H_\Gamma$ one needs its derived nerve on the free arities to have the finite globular-sum Segal equivalences, and a fibrant presheaf representing it on the raw site to return a raw-algebra comparison to $Y$ with prescribed-boundary lifting as in the cylinder construction there. Given this input, take the derived mapping presheaf out of $M(P)$ into $Y$. The comparison just proved identifies its restriction along raw substitutions with that raw nerve. The fixed-object row rigidification, extension over the mixed arities, and cylinder prescriptions of Response 222 now apply using items 1–5. They give the binary $A,U$ action. Lifting the generators of $\Gamma$ along the first projection of the raw comparison, evaluating the proposed surface cell, and lifting it at the already specified boundary along the second gives a raw inhabitant of the old type. The repository's quoted raw-model theorem concerns the ordinary and two-tier Gray kernels. The arity resolution above by itself proves neither this derived-nerve input for every $E$ nor its full-support counterpart. ## Researcher Response 10

**Every individual ordinary surface in Q1(b,c) reflects inhabitation through dimension five on every finite 3-computad in which the spatial input and output paths of each 2- and 3-generator are nonempty.** The same proof treats all eight binary surfaces and both published surfaces separately. Generators may repeat, both 2-faces of a 3-generator may be identities, and the number of events in the strict 3-label is unrestricted. At a label admitting no simultaneous three-marker circuit, reflection holds in **all** dimensions. In particular if this last condition holds for every label, one may attach arbitrary generators of ranks at least four and still obtain reflection through degree five.

### Which exchanges can be made identities?

Write $T$ for the cubical transition presentation at fixed 1-boundaries. A cube is a planar circuit in which some boxes are marked by generating 3-cells, with a choice of source or target expansion on each face. All the lower marked 2-computads have nonempty input and output at every gate, so are bubble-free. All the circuits are rigid relative to their ordered boundary. Consequently the mapping space at a strict 3-label $c$ is the cube-chain complex $E(c)$: its vertices are serial histories, its edges specify *which* simultaneous two-marker circuit exchanges two successive events, and its two-dimensional relations are disjoint-block squares and three-marker hexagons. Its cells are products of permutohedra, with one factor for each simultaneous circuit in sequence. This presentation uses only homotopicality of the lower marked computads, not relative coherence of the 3-computad. [Transition presentation and edge realization](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#cells-of-the-mapping-space)

Inside $T$ keep the cubes in which no directed wire path goes from one marker to another, allowing paths to pass through any number of ordinary gates. Denote the resulting cubical set by $T_o$. Expanding a marker can only restrict the possible paths through its position, so this is closed under faces. Call an edge of $E(c)$ outer when its two-marker factor is in $T_o$. The subgroupoid of $\Pi_1E(c)$ generated by outer edges is **thin**: it contains at most one morphism with each pair of endpoints.

Here is a signed-link proof. At an ordinary vertex $D$ use the width of each wire given by the number of directed external-input to external-output paths through it. The widths are positive, and incoming and outgoing sums at each gate agree. They give each wire and gate a fixed interval of unit lanes, independent of serialization. A signed germ determines a consecutive block on each lane of its marker interval in $D$, including the specified gap on an idle through wire. The face expansion specifies these blocks uniquely by rigidity. In a joint outer two-marker frame the markers are incomparable in the wire order and can be executed in a single slice: take all their strict predecessors first, then the two markers. Linear extensions give serializations because enabled gates on distinct wires commute. Thus the two intervals are disjoint, also when either expansion is an identity.

For a clique of outer germs, replace their prescribed blocks by boxes. The intervals of the boxes are pairwise disjoint. To see that the replacements can all be serialized, order intersecting intervals of exterior gates by their old lane order, and order each box against each intersecting exterior gate by its single-germ frame. Form the graph of these comparisons. Any directed cycle of minimal length at least four has a chord (choose a vertex with least right endpoint). Any triangle includes at most one box and has the orders in the corresponding single frame or in $D$. Thus there is no directed cycle. A linear extension expands to the gate order of $D$ on every lane, including the prescribed cuts on idle wires. At each input it therefore exposes precisely the prescribed word of concrete ports. Two linear extensions differ by exchanges on disjoint intervals, giving the same circuit. If two boxes were connected by a directed path, choose two consecutive boxes on such a path and expand all other boxes. The exterior segment survives in the unique two-germ frame, contradicting its being outer. The constructed simplex is unique and its germs are distinct (the intervals are nonempty). This proves the signed links of $T_o$ are simplicial and flag, also for loops with the same two face expansions. [Local widths and port reconstruction](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#positive-3-computads-with-no-matching-idle-boundary-paths)

In the universal cover of a cubical component of $T_o$, the time orientation of opposite edges agrees in every square. Each hyperplane therefore has one positive side. A directed vertex path is geodesic, crossing no hyperplane twice. Between two given lifted endpoints the directed cube chains are the ordered partitions into independent steps of the finite order ideal interval of separating hyperplanes. Their complex is contractible by the order-ideal attachment argument for the extreme hom. Outer exchanges preserve the lift of the endpoint and all cube chains there project back, with their subdivisions. An outer loop of serial histories thus bounds already in the cube chains of $T_o$, hence in $E(c)$. This proves thinness. It does **not** assert that two specified exchanges in $T$ coincide when they have the same serial endpoints. [Interval and cube-chain argument](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#positive-3-computads-with-no-matching-idle-boundary-paths)

### A surface model that retains all other tracks

Use the cartesian enrichment in Gray-categories of round-1 Response 42. The objects are the original objects, the arrows are paths, and the 2-cells are planar diagrams. At fixed 2-poles take the disjoint union of $\Pi_1 E(c)$ over the strict 3-labels there. Its objects are serial histories and its arrows are tracks. Identify histories connected by outer exchanges and transport tracks at their two ends by the outer paths. Thinness proves that this quotient groupoid is equivalent to the original by a functor fully faithful on every original pair of endpoints: fix representatives of the two classes and transport to them by the unique outer tracks. The resulting objects and arrows give degrees three and four of $M$.

We detail the composition checks since identifying *every* exchange in the construction would invalidate the conclusion. At degree two the two compositions are juxtaposition of planar diagrams and stacking them. At a fixed object pair, the local hom 2-categories compose histories by concatenation and tracks by whiskering with histories and composing paths. The two ways of pasting tracks agree by the disjoint-block squares. Pre- and post-whiskering by a fixed planar diagram stack it against the circuit of each event or switch. This preserves outerness: a path exiting through the common top, or entering through the common bottom, cannot return through that boundary. Thus the operations descend to the endpoint quotients. Their strict unit and associativity are those of the circuit operations and concatenation.

For local Gray composition the invertible interchanger of two elementary events in consecutive vertically stacked diagrams is the edge with *that stacked two-marker diagram*. For histories move one event block past the other using its rectangle of individual switches. Each next pair of events has its two local states fixed by the two executed prefixes. Different sweeps of this rectangle differ by squares for disjoint adjacent switches. Splitting a history subdivides the rectangle and an empty block contributes its identity. For naturality with a track it suffices to move one elementary switch past one event in the other factor: stacking the switch's entire two-marker circuit and the event gives one three-marker circuit, whose hexagon gives the equation. Fixed prefixes and suffixes whisker this equation. In particular it applies to a change of representative by an outer switch; with the opposite operand stationary the switch stays outer, so the same equation descends by endpoint transport. For three successive local factors, the two iterated compositions agree on the elementary crossings: a crossing of nonadjacent factors has the intervening diagram stationary, and the interchanger of the remaining block splits by the rectangle rule. The two reorderings of three elementary events use the hexagon of their stacked three-marker circuit. These checks give the strict hom 2-categories, cubical local composition, and its associative and unital Gray tensor functors.

For the exterior cartesian functor juxtapose circuits, performing the left history first and the right history second. Any interchange between these two factors is outer, including after stationary stacking, so the two serial orders give equal quotient histories. On tracks, whisker the left and right switches by the stationary opposite factor and use disjoint-block squares. Moving a switch past an event in the opposite factor is the hexagon of the juxtaposed two-marker and one-marker circuits, with the two cross comparisons outer. This proves independence of the order of the factors on tracks after endpoint transport and compatibility with concatenation. Stacking juxtaposed stationary diagrams commutes literally with juxtaposing stacks. To check strict preservation of a local Gray interchanger, take the elementary array of two stacked events on the left and two on the right. Juxtapose the two stacked two-marker circuits. This is a *single four-marker circuit in the full $T$*, whether or not the vertically adjacent markers are wire-comparable. Executing the two local comparisons separately and executing the comparison of the two juxtaposed rows are paths in this one permutohedron, with the initial and final serial representatives matched by outer switches. The paths agree there by its squares and hexagons. With one or more stationary positions the same check is in a lower-dimensional permutohedron. For arbitrary lists, split interchangers into their elementary rectangles; commuting the disjoint switches and moving a single switch past an opposite event are exactly the preceding square and hexagon verifications. Juxtaposing three factors in either bracketing performs their histories in the same order, with the same whiskerings on each generating track. This proves the required strict exterior associativity, unit and preservation of Gray structure. In particular the proof does not require four arbitrary successive events of a history to admit a simultaneous presentation.

### Reflection at the written boundary

Interpret the given declarations in the published model $K_o(M)$ of round-1 Response 42 by their paths, planar diagrams and one-marker histories. At the actual weak attaching faces these have the required values since everything through degree two is evaluated strictly. The map from the raw free realization at fixed lower poles induces on $E(c)$ the functor to the nerve of the track groupoid just constructed. To check the assertion on fundamental groupoids, choose unary lifts and bracket their composites at the serial vertices. For each edge serialize its specified lower two-marker circuit into ordinary and marker rows. The generic two-row comparison maps to the local Gray interchanger of those two event rows, with the indicated stationary rows stacked or whiskered onto it. This is precisely the class of the original edge, including when the rows are causally independent. Indeed in the column interpretation the triangle for the chosen serial order is an identity and the other triangle is the compositor of the two row slots. Their composite is that interchanger or its inverse according to orientation. Fitting the row terms at the four corners and unary sides contributes only the empty and one-event tracks; their source fibres are points. Rebracketing a serial prefix or suffix amounts to concatenation. The edges generate the fundamental groupoid, so this prescription and the fully faithful endpoint quotient above prove injectivity on relative path classes. The lower poles may be fixed by the discrete lower globe comparison for the transition presentation. [Raw representatives at literal faces](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#representing-edges-and-faces-by-terms) · [Two surface actions, comparison span and calculation of a switch](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-42)

For a binary surface lift the same generator assignment through the raw trivial fibration from the comparison span to $K_o(M)$ and project to the binary model. An old 5-type inhabited there has equal 4-tracks. The commuting square defining each 4-cell of the span transports that equality to equality at the prescribed $K_o(M)$ ends (the endpoint isomorphisms are the same for the parallel old faces). For a published surface evaluate directly in $K_o(M)$. In both cases the raw paths are thus homotopic relative to the old ends in $E(c)$. Raw globe-boundary comparison returns a filler at the written type. Together with the ordinary through-four theorem this proves the degree bound.

If a label admits no three-marker factor in any cube chain, its mapping space is a cube complex: each nontrivial factor is an exchange interval. At a serial vertex each signed direction specifies an adjacent pair of positions and its actual chart. Two directions in a square use disjoint position pairs. Conversely any collection of such disjoint choices has a unique common cube by concatenating the chosen factors and the intervening serial steps. This proves the signed links simplicial and flag even if charts share endpoints. The universal cover is contractible, so all higher homotopy groups vanish. Parallel raw higher faces over this label therefore have fillers from degree six on. If all labels satisfy the hypothesis, the raw map to $K_o(M)$ is a weak equivalence: the lower flags agree, the components are indexed by the same labels, the fundamental groupoids agree as above, and both sides are 1-types on each such component. The span and the base-change theorem then give the stated degree-five conclusion with arbitrary higher declarations. More generally that last argument applies when all $E(c)$ are aspherical by any proof. The arbitrary positive case above asserts faithfulness at degree five and does not require that additional hypothesis. [Base change with specified faces](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-32)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_spatial_frames.py`. With five inputs through three gates the checker reconstructs 5,272 signed two-dimensional and 176 three-dimensional outer corners, uniquely, and retains both edges on all 33 circular two-event components. With four inputs through four gates it checks 36,300, 1,480 and 16 corners of dimensions two through four and retains both edges on all 128 circles. Separate identity-face loops on overlapping single- and double-wire intervals test both signs up to a six-dimensional corner. On the generic simultaneous four-slot circuit all 66 sorting routes (over the 24 start orders) are connected within their respective endpoint pairs by 26 square and 20 hexagon moves. These checks exercise the finite links, endpoint distinction and interchange calculations. The unbounded conclusion follows from the proofs above. ## Researcher Response 11

**The degree-five conclusion of Response 10 holds in arbitrary higher extensions of its positive 3-skeleton, including directed 4- and 5-declarations at composite raw faces.** The seam and asphericity restrictions are unnecessary for this conclusion. Thus a first failure of an individual ordinary surface in Q1(b,c) in degree five would require a 2- or 3-generator with empty spatial input or output on the dependency-closed support. The bound for the joined surface is different.

### A finite-degree version of base change

Fix one of the individual ordinary surfaces, $r\ge1$, and $q=r+2$. Let $B=X_{\le r}$, and suppose a raw map $f:H_B\to D$ lifts every specified target cell at every prescribed raw boundary through degree $q$ (including objects). Suppose that $D$ is weakly equivalent to the reduct of a model $Q$ of the chosen surface. Then the surface reflects inhabitation through degree $q$ on $X$, with arbitrary higher attachments. The hypotheses concern **relative** lifts: injectivity on an invariant of boundaries alone would not imply them.

First construct a free raw extension $C$ of $H_B$ over $D$. Adjoin only generators of ranks $q+1$ and above. In each rank, for every parallel pair already formed one degree below and every target cell at their $D$-images, adjoin a generator at that pair sent to that target. Proceed in increasing rank. The extension may be infinite; raw terms and attaching maps are finitary. In ordinary raw syntax a head of degree $i$ has arity of height at most $i$ by its full-support strict cell; its faces and the types of its arguments have lower rank. Thus adding generators of degree greater than $i$ leaves the raw cells and their typings through degree $i$ identical. Lower lifts persist and the new generators give the higher lifts, so $C\to D$ is a globular trivial fibration. There is a raw weak equivalence $C\to UQ$: use cofibrancy of the free extension and fibrancy of the target to represent the composite equivalence. For the explicit span in round-1 Response 42 one can simply lift the assignment of $C$ through the trivial fibration onto the published model and project to the binary model.

Form $X'=C\amalg_{H_B}H_X$ and $Y=UQ\amalg_C X'$. Then $X'\to Y$ is a raw weak equivalence by left properness since $C\to X'$ is the base change of the cellular inclusion $H_B\to H_X$. Equivalently $X'$ is obtained from $H_X$ by the high attachments of $C$, retaining exactly its raw cells through $q$. The target $Y$ is the free relative extension of $UQ$ by the declarations of $X$ above $r$, attached at their transported faces. In particular all its cells through $r$ belong to the base. These statements about the pushouts hold even if the old declarations above $r$ also occur in the ranks of the added generators: the latter attach in $C$, hence use only $B$ and earlier added cells.

Apply the two-degree construction of round-1 Responses 31–32 to **this $Y$**, with generic lower bases the free surface algebras on $P_{\le r}$ as there. This part of the construction requires the relative extension of the surface base and the generic arity comparisons, both just specified. It keeps the base through $r$, identifies mutually reachable $(r+1)$-cells, and represents a directed $(r+2)$-cell by its order relation. Any surface inhabitant at the assignment of $X$ therefore supplies a raw cell in $Y$ at its prescribed old faces, taking the directed witness at the original representatives. The weak equivalence $X'\to Y$ reflects existence at those faces by the globe-boundary test. The resulting raw cell in $X'$ has degree at most $q$, so is already in $H_X$. This is the only place the actual-base weak equivalence in the original proof had to be replaced. ### A finite search for fixed faces on globular generators

There is a uniform reduction of an individual old inhabitation query on a finite globular set $Z$ to **finitely many** fixed-boundary queries on trees. For $n=1$ take the single vertex slot. If there are no top slots the only extension is identity padding. ## Researcher Response 14

**For each individual ordinary surface, degree-five conservativity holds in every finite context if all spatial inputs of its 2- and 3-generators are nonempty. It also holds if all their spatial outputs are nonempty.** The opposite ends may be empty and declarations in ranks four and above are arbitrary. Consequently a degree-five counterexample to Q1(b,c) must involve **both** an empty spatial input and an empty spatial output among its 2- and 3-generators on the support of the boundary and the proposed inhabitant. A single generator may have both. The no-three-marker, all-output-degree criterion of Response 10 for a 3-computad extends to these one-sided hypotheses.

Work in the common-input case. All ordinary and marked gates consume a wire. Every component of a nonempty circuit meets the input boundary: follow input wires backwards. All lower marked 2-computads are bubble-free, and circuits are rigid fixing ports. Thus the cubical presentation $T$ and the fibre formula $E(c)$ used in Response 10 apply. The relevant outer cubes now consist of the circuits in which a single serial cut exposes the input blocks of **all** markers as mutually disjoint consecutive words: all markers can occur in the same layer. Expanding a marker preserves this property by executing its whole expansion while the other input blocks are stationary. Wire incomparability alone would give a wrong test. For example delete the middle of three wires, then merge the two exterior ones. The merge uses no output of the deletion, but its two inputs cannot be adjacent before the deletion. This distinction concerns the strict spatial product in the model.

### Ordered lanes for disappearing wires

In an expanded ordinary corner $D$, end a maximal directed path either at an output port of the whole circuit or at an input port of a gate with empty output. The width of a wire is the number of paths from external inputs to those ends that contain it. All widths are positive; a gate with nonempty output has equal total incoming and outgoing widths. Give the input wires consecutive finite lists of unit lanes. A gate takes the ordered concatenation of the lists of its inputs and divides it in order among its outputs by their widths. A gate with empty output discards the list. The surviving lists are ordered and disjoint, but may have gaps. Take the *hull* of a gate to be the closed interval of integer lanes from the first to the last of its input list, including the gaps. Executing disjoint enabled steps in either order gives identical lists and hulls. Thus every serialization of a given planar circuit gives each ordinary gate and each concrete wire the same lists. A signed marker germ obtains its input list on $D$ by serializing its one-marker frame and expanding that marker as a block; do the lane calculation on the expansion. Each exchange of independent events in that frame exchanges blocks on disjoint live input words. Since every output position of a block derives from its own input word, the lists and hull of the marker are invariant as well.

Two observations give the port reconstruction. Gates enabled on disjoint input subwords at one cut have disjoint hulls, since the live lane lists are strictly ordered. Conversely, two consecutive gates in a valid serialization whose hulls are disjoint can be interchanged. The second gate uses no output of the first; all its inputs are already there. If two consecutive wires of its input block were separated by a wire just before the first gate, that intervening wire would have to be consumed by the first gate and its lane list would lie inside the second hull. This is impossible. The two input blocks are consequently disjoint and enabled together. The same observations apply to a germ as a single block: all its internal gates and surviving output ports use lanes from its own hull. These assertions explain why the hull must include gaps.

### The outer signed links

Fix a clique of outer germs at $D$. Their input hulls are pairwise disjoint, as seen by expanding a common layer for each pair. The gates of their prescribed expansions therefore are disjoint. Group these gates into their proposed marker blocks, retaining also the individual exterior gates. For every intersecting pair of hulls order the two participants by their order in $D$ for two ordinary gates, or in the corresponding one-marker frame for a marker and an exterior gate. These orders are intrinsic: an interchange at which the participants change order would require the two to be enabled together with disjoint input blocks, hence to have disjoint hulls. There are no comparisons between different markers at this stage. The comparison graph is acyclic. A shortest cycle of length greater than three has an interval chord, and a triangle involves at most one marker, so is already consistently ordered in an actual frame.

Take a linear extension. Replacing each marker by its prescribed consecutive expansion produces a serialization of $D$: the relative order of any intersecting pair of ordinary hulls is the original one. This follows from the single-marker frame if one member belongs to the expansion; different markers have disjoint hulls. One may move from an original serialization to this expanded order by transpositions of adjacent disjoint hulls, which are legal by the preceding observation. At the start of each expanded block its exact marker input word is available, including all its idle wires. To check this last point, compare with its single-germ serialization: the orders of every exterior gate meeting the marker hull agree. All intervening changes on disjoint hulls preserve the wires, their order, and adjacency within the marker hull. Equivalently, retain the marker as one step, expand all other blocks in the proposed order, and compare it with the single-germ schedule by transpositions of disjoint hulls. Membership of an internal gap in the prescribed word is thereby checked *before* replacing the block. Replacing disjoint blocks then exposes exactly their intended output words, even when an output is empty. Any two linear extensions give the same resulting circuit since they differ by such disjoint exchanges.

Finally no directed comparison chain can run from one proposed marker to another. Take a segment between consecutive markers on such a chain. All its interior nodes are exterior ordinary gates. The same intersection orders on that segment hold in the original outer frame of just those two germs: expand each of its markers to compare with the relevant one-marker frame. A common layer in the two-germ frame contradicts the strict order imposed by the chain. The clique is thus an antichain in the reconstructed order and can be put in one layer by executing all its strict predecessors first. This constructs its unique outer cube. Distinct germs in such a cube have disjoint nonempty hulls. These arguments prove the signed links simplicial and flag even for endomorphic marker faces. The directed-interval proof of Response 10 now gives a thin groupoid of outer tracks.

The model and the generator-assignment proof of Responses 10–11 use precisely the following properties of outer tracks: juxtaposed events have an outer switch, stacking or juxtaposing stationary diagrams preserves it, and transport between outer-related representatives is unique. All three hold for actual common layers. The full $T$ still supplies the stacked Gray interchangers and their square, hexagon and four-marker comparisons. This gives specified lifts through rank five and the finite-degree base-change theorem applies. If all outputs are nonempty, read each lower serialization from the output boundary, ending paths at external inputs or at gates with empty input. **In the four fixed settings, a tree with generating 3-cells in two different outer columns cannot be the bound arity of a full-support head in any dimension.** This holds in raw syntax and after any interchange-free binary conversion. In particular the strict comparison for Q1(a) can fail even when restricted to strict cells having full projected Gray support. There is also an old 4-type whose suspension has a direct raw inhabitant although the original type has none.

### The inaccessible face of the tensor of two 2-globes

Let $B=\Sigma D^2\vee\Sigma D^2$ and use the six 3-schedules of Response 13. In its long Gray hom the tensor of the two globe atoms gives a global 5-cell. Its parallel 4-faces are the two routes in the Gray diamond from $BCA$ to $ACB$. One goes via the class of $CBA,CAB$, the other via the class of $ABC,BAC$. The second is the Gray image of the strict composite $h(A,B)$ followed by the right prism. The two routes have distinct Gray tableaux: the first uses the right prism at the initial left version and the left prism at the initial right version, while the second uses the left prism at the final right version and the right prism at the final left version. Equivalently the two signed tensor facets contain different contextual prism basis elements. The native strict 5-generator $W(A,B)$ has these two projected faces and projects to the full tensor atom. Now the entire degree-four syntax takes values in $D(B)$. The only $D(B)$ path from $BCA$ to $ACB$ is
$$
 BCA\longrightarrow CBA\longrightarrow CAB\longrightarrow ACB .
$$
Its Gray image is the first route. The inner square in the middle projects to an identity. The alternative first step to $BAC$ leads to a sink in $D(B)$. This argument also excludes a surface 4-term projecting to the second route when $I$ is absent, since the same $D(B)$ invariant holds for that interpretation. It follows by taking the corresponding written 4-face that no raw or interchange-free surface 5-term on $B$ projects to the full Gray atom. Higher identity elevations of that atom are excluded by taking iterated faces. In degrees below five a Gray value cannot have full support on $B$ since the top tensor basis element is in global degree five. A full-support value in degree five or above on this arity is the total or its identity elevation, by the tree support characterization of §1.1. Hence *no term* on $B$ has full Gray image support. An admitted full-support head would itself give such a term on the identity substitution. The variable rule does admit, for example, the identity-image coherence on $BCA$ with itself: the typed schedule uses both 3-generators and all their faces.

Let $P=\bigvee_i\Sigma\Theta_i$, and choose outer columns $i<j$ with local height at least two. In each retain one generating local 2-globe. ## Researcher Response 16

**There is an exact Q2 classification for the join in Q1(c) on iterated planar substitutions, including presentations with births and deaths.** Start with any finite ordinary 2-computad $P$. At each generating 2-cell $g$, either keep it or replace it by a twofold suspension of another nonempty finite 2-computad at the actual attaching poles of $g$. Repeat inside the inserted presentations a finite number of times. Equivalently put a point at every undecorated slot. Every block is allowed arbitrary weak 1-attachments. A bubble in a block means a nonempty planar history from the empty path at an object back to that empty path in the **block's own** strict category.

* The join is conservative in all output degrees exactly when every block is bubble-free. In that case even conversion by full ordinary strict-image equality is conservative.
* If a shallowest block with a bubble has depth $d$ (the root has depth zero), the least failing output dimension for the join is exactly $2d+4$. This applies even if the offending block is further decorated. Thus each of these even first-failure dimensions occurs.
* With the finite decomposition given, all-dimensional recognition is $\Pi^0_1$-complete. A failure can be enumerated by searching the finitely many blocks for a positive return. Hardness holds already at the root by Response 1. A lower bound can also be placed at any fixed depth by wrapping the hard instance in single 2-discs.

### Substitution at a bubble-free planar block

For a bubble-free $P$, the occurrence product formula for inserting arbitrary finite presentations $K_g$ at $g$ holds even if both births and deaths occur in $P$. No condition on $K_g$ is needed for this formula. To see where the positivity hypothesis of the general substitution theorem can be lifted in this dimension, first replace each $g$ by a discrete set $S_g$ of parallel colours. A strict 2-diagram $w$ has a rigid set of gate occurrences $O(w)$. In fact, a component missing the entire boundary would on its own be a nonidentity empty-to-empty planar diagram. Every component therefore meets a boundary wire, and fixing its incident ordered ports propagates uniquely through it. The perimeter together with the anchored components is connected, so the planar embedding and region labels are retained. Thus, above the actual diagram $w$, colours are chosen independently on the individual gates, even at repeated labels. An equality of diagrams transports them by the unique boundary-fixing incidence isomorphism. Vertical and exterior composition join the occurrence lists by disjoint union, with no choice of a matching of repeated gates. A through wire has no occurrence to colour. [Planar rigidity and the bubble-free realization theorem](../answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md)

The coloured planar presentation $P[S]$ is again bubble-free: forgetting colours carries a positive history to one of the same length. For finite discrete colours both free realizations are therefore their strict nerves. In particular the discrete-colour pullback required in the substitution proof is the actual pullback over $NF(P)$, with the occurrence fibres just calculated. Infinite discrete colour sets follow by the filtered union on finite subsets per gate: the presentations are cellular unions, every finite strict diagram uses finitely many colours, and filtered colimits in spaces preserve the finite products in the Segal and flag calculations. Equivalently check the discrete formula before horizontal completion: the 1-skeleton is unchanged, the completed horizontal objects are the indicated gaunt 2-categories, and the map on the lower flags is the identity. Full faithfulness on top homs then gives the same coloured formula for horizontal Segal localization.

Now apply the space-valued-colour argument of the substitution theorem. In the presheaf pushout for the attachments, at inner rectangle $R$ the parallel colours are the spaces $X_{K_g}(R)$. Resolve these by simplicial diagrams of discrete sets. Geometric realization preserves the finite products indexed by the occurrences, and the horizontal spine partitions those occurrences among its rectangles. The localized value over $w$ is therefore the product of the inner values over its individual gates. The inner Segal and completeness tests hold factorwise. At outer identities the occurrence product is empty and the lower flags are unchanged, giving outer completeness. On the strict side the same formula uses $NF(K_g)$; the universal property follows by evaluating any planar expression with an inner cell at each occurrence, and composing componentwise in the inner directions. This proves the comparison at fixed lower flags, including its naturality for maps of the inserted presentations. In particular, above **one** occurrence at $g$ it restricts to the original comparison of $K_g$, with dimensions shifted by two. Arbitrary old weak poles are handled by the derived globe-boundary comparison. This proof uses the planar theorem on the outer colours only, so it remains valid when a child has noncontractible fibres. Two consequences of the fixed-flag description are worth specifying. First, a prescribed-boundary strict lifting range through degree $k$ for *each* child gives a range through $k+2$ for the result. Through degree two use the coloured planar calculation at inner objects and the graph calculation below it. At higher degrees, the specified two 2-poles must project to the same outer diagram, and the inner strict label and its boundary split over its concrete occurrences. Each component supplies its relative disc at the prescribed poles. Their product is a geometric relative disc over the specified label, which the raw unit and boundary test realizes at the written faces. One can also check the statement on the restrictions of the derived disc mapping spaces to the boundary mapping spaces: over the fixed outer diagram these are products of the corresponding inner restrictions, so nonemptiness of every required fibre is preserved. Second, at a single outer occurrence any relative disc between suspended old faces projects to an inner relative disc between the original faces. All higher cells between these poles stay over that outer diagram. Hence a *specific* missing old comparison survives at that slot, irrespective of activity at other slots. ### The least failing dimension

If all blocks are bubble-free, induction from the leaves makes every strict label lift at every prescribed boundary. Strict soundness of the join gives reflection. If the root block has a bubble, select one object of each decoration to realize the original $P$ by a raw generator substitution in the result. The raw diagonal on its positive bubble maps to a diagonal that is an identity in the join, by Response 1. Its missing raw comparison still has no filler: collapsing each decoration to a point gives a retraction on free realizations, fixing that section. This retraction exists without bubble-freeness, by the suspension–fixed-hom adjunction on the defining cellular pushouts applied to the map of each inserted realization to the point. Thus a relative disc at the selected raw faces would project to the forbidden one over $P$. Equivalently, to realize the collapse in raw syntax, assign each higher declaration of a decoration a filler on the abstract 2-disc of its selected generator, then specialize to its actual poles. All proposed faces project to the unique required iterated identity at that disc, whose ordinary strict comparison lifts prescribed boundaries. The collapse fixes the selected 2-generator. Hence the failure is in degree four. The ordinary strict lifting through degree three on arbitrary computads gives the lower bound there. [Planar diagonal](#researcher-response-1) · [Ordinary three-dimensional prescribed-boundary lifting](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#strictifying-the-fixed-homs)

For positive depth, every ancestor down to the shallowest bad block is bubble-free. At any such block the individual child ranges combine by the product calculation, adding two to the minimum. At depth $d$ every child context, even a decorated bad block, still has ordinary strict lifting through three. Induction up to the root thus gives strict lifting through $2d+3$. For the reverse bound use the missing raw diagonal from that bad block, taking one occurrence in each ancestor. The fixed-flag single-occurrence calculation reflects raw relative discs down through the ancestors, so its $2d$-fold suspension at the actual poles still has no raw filler. Its joined filler is the iterated suspended scalar-diagonal calculation: the two local cuts in the binary square are shifted together, the strict-unit splice and endo calculation are the suspended ones, and substitution gives the assertion at the written bubble. These are precisely the shifted equations used in Response 4. Consequently the join actually fails at output degree $2d+4$; the calculation has not merely exhibited a failure of strict-image lifting there. [Lifted diagonal equations](#researcher-response-4)

For example, placing the one-generator scalar block at the end of a tower of $d$ 2-discs gives the asserted least dimensions. The same criterion also decides the mathematical question for any finite context with both faces of each generator above degree two literal variables: group its 2-generators by their common literal type and read the higher data over each group as a finite globular set. The bottom objects of that globular set are the parallel 2-generators, and incidence in every higher rank stays within the group. Such a decoration has its strict nerve as free realization and lifts all prescribed strict boundaries by the globular comparison. The same product and root-retraction proof applies, so the join fails in degree four exactly when the 2-skeleton has a bubble; otherwise it is conservative in every degree. Folding a group of parallel generators to one representative and choosing a representative back both preserve positivity of a planar history. ## Researcher Response 17

**All ten individual ordinary surfaces are conservative in every dimension on an iterated planar substitution of Response 16 whenever every nonterminal block is bubble-free. The terminal 2-computads may have arbitrary bubbles.** This gives presentations with births and deaths, unbounded generating rank, and repeated copies of the same noncontractible factor at different occurrences. In this class the individual surfaces remain conservative even when their join first fails in an arbitrarily high even degree.

There is a model-level closure theorem. Let $P$ be any bubble-free finite planar block, and replace its generating 2-cells by twofold suspensions of arbitrary finite presentations $K_g$ at their actual attaching poles. Fix either insertion or the full binary surface with unary disc removal (the latter implies all eight binary results). Suppose the free raw realization of each $K_g$ is weakly equivalent to the reduct of a model $Q_g$ of this same surface. Then the free raw realization of the substitution is weakly equivalent to a reduct of a model of that surface. The published strict-unit conclusion follows already from insertion. The two initial model comparisons for every finite planar leaf, including scalar leaves, are the planar models of round-1 Responses 26 and 32. [Planar starting models](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-26) · [Disc rule and suspended surface models](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-11)

### The outer diagrams and the local coordinates

Keep the vertices and paths of $P$. In degrees $2+j$ use a strict planar diagram $w$ and one $j$-cell of $Q_{g(o)}$ for each concrete gate occurrence $o$ in $w$. At $j=0$ its poles are the two paths of $w$; for $j>0$ take the two faces in each coordinate, retaining $w$. Forgetting coordinates gives a globular projection to the identity extension of $F(P)$. The points of an empty product supply the unique cell over that diagram at every higher degree. We define operations with this strict projection. The issue is that a single labelled argument can itself contain several copies of a gate and an actual birth may have no input wire through which one could cut the ambient context.

Write a generic tree as a sequence of suspended columns, each column in turn a sequence of suspended subtrees $T_{ij}$. On any labelling, each such position has one planar diagram $w_{ij}$: the 2-labels over it are connected by its higher slots, which preserve the underlying diagram. Different positions in one column are stacked; the columns are juxtaposed. This is the ordinary strict planar evaluation of the projected total. Its occurrences are the disjoint union of the occurrences in these $w_{ij}$, even for repeated diagrams or empty subwords, by the rigidity proof in Response 16. Given a gate $o$ in the result, recover its unique $(i,j)$ and its own occurrence $o'$ in $w_{ij}$. Extract the $o'$ coordinate of every label over $T_{ij}$. This gives a globular tuple there in $Q_{g(o)}$.

Evaluate the original head at this coordinate by the **two successive arity cuts** selecting $i$, then $j$, with that extracted tuple. Equivalently label the original tree in the double suspension model of $Q_{g(o)}$: at the first cut use stationary poles outside the selected column and at the second cut use stationary data outside its selected row. In the twice-crossing subtree use the extracted tuple. For path operations use ordinary path composition. In degree two this prescription simply retains the specified object from the selected 2-label. In degree greater than two it uses the restricted head at its actual generic faces. The cuts are taken on the *arity*. Neither an object colouring of $P$ nor a through wire at $g$ is required. [Restriction of generic heads, binary laws and disc](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#restriction-of-operations) · [Insertion, including root splicing](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-12)

Here are the substitution and equality checks. Over the tree of a tuple the ordinary 1-value of a subexpression selects an interval of outer columns, and in each traversed column its 2-value selects an interval of rows and the relevant 2-position in each row. This is the height-two truncation of the usual strict tree formula, applied to its two boundary paths when the subexpression is higher-dimensional. For an expression read at a chosen occurrence in its value, restrict to those intervals. If an outer interval misses the first cut, evaluating that piece in the suspended model gives stationary data; the same holds at the second cut in the retained column. On a traversed row the chosen occurrence has a unique antecedent among the diagrams of the substituted arguments. Expanding a second tuple refines this antecedent: both ways of splitting the occurrence select the same gate in the flattened planar paste. This is disjoint union for composition together with uniqueness of the planar identification, and includes identity-labelled branches with no gate to select. Recursing over a head and its boundary expressions therefore gives precisely evaluation in the one double-suspension labelling just described. In particular the lower faces of the answer are the previously interpreted faces. For strict labels at higher local times the same antecedent is used on both faces, since erasing the decorations sends that entire change to an identity on $w_{ij}$.

For a word or head equation over a common generic labelling, both sides have the same generic ordinary strict value. Use its interval supports to select $(i,j),o'$ once. Evaluating both sides at this coordinate is evaluating that rule on a single tuple in the double suspension model; hence the coordinates agree. For insertion use the joined arity and its interior and exterior substitutions as the common generic labelling. A root subdivision selects the unique antecedent in the appropriate subcolumn, and a splice in that subcolumn uses the second-cut calculation. If the result has no occurrences, equality follows from the empty product and the shared strict value. Congruence at converted head types follows from evaluation in the same suspension model at the interpreted faces. These checks also apply to disc removal in the auxiliary binary surface, whose single-crossing disc returns its actual argument. Together they define the claimed surface algebra $A$.

### Comparison with the raw substitution

Choose generator assignments realizing raw weak equivalences $H_{K_g}\to UQ_g$, using cofibrancy and fibrancy if the equivalences were supplied as zigzags. Assign the new declarations in the substituted computad to these coordinates over the single gate $g$. On the private suspended attaching terms both cuts recover the original local heads, so these assignments have exactly their written types.

To check the resulting raw map, fix the lower poles. In the twice-fixed hom of $A$ at a pair of paths, the summands are indexed by diagrams $w$ between those paths, and the raw algebra of each summand is literally the product of $UQ_{g(o)}$ over its gates. Indeed the hom operations are the twice-suspended raw heads; applying the two cuts to one of those heads gives the original head, on its original tuple in each coordinate. Distinct diagrams have no crossing 3-cell. Realization of these products computes the product of realized inner homs: products of fibrant raw algebras compute homotopy products, and the raw-model comparison identifies their derived nerves. A disjoint union here has disjoint homs in all positive ranks. The object and path flags have the ordinary discrete comparison: an equivalence between paths would project to a planar equivalence, hence to an identity by the gate count. Over a planar identity there are no coordinates to supply any extra equivalences.

On the source, the relative pushout and occurrence computation of Response 16 give the corresponding product of the free inner realizations. To identify the **map** between these products, choose a serialization of $w$. Composing its gate corollas and its stationary wires gives the occurrence-product equivalence on the source: at every inner rectangle of the proof the corollas contribute the independent factors and the composition is their displayed disjoint-union identification. In the target the same pasting takes the tuple from each corolla to its own coordinate. For the two elementary outer products, cutting away the opposite operand selects the moving entry; at its own degree the resulting disc operation returns that entry by disc removal. Stationary lower-dimensional inputs have only their identity padding. This computation applies to a whole tuple on an inner globular tree: all its cells are treated coordinatewise and a suspended inner head retains its original operation. Thus these pastes on inner homs are the product inclusions with all other factors stationary, or jointly the concatenation of the factors. At a single corolla the map is exactly the chosen child comparison. Naturality for the chosen composition of corollas identifies the map on the hom over $w$ with the product of the child comparisons, up to the compositional homotopy of the realization. This proves it a weak equivalence, at the lower flags as well as at every twice-fixed hom. [Fixed-hom comparison for a positional product](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#independent-edge-computads-and-repeated-positions) · [Planar occurrence product](#researcher-response-16)

A surface filler evaluated in $A$ at the original declarations therefore gives a raw filler at the old faces by the globe-boundary test. The model can itself serve as a child in the same construction, proving the iterated assertion. It also supplies the base comparison of round-1 Response 32 for arbitrary subsequent attachments starting above the generated skeleton, with that theorem's two-degree bound. For a bubbling nonterminal planar block, the occurrence decomposition used in this proof need not exist.

## Researcher Response 18

**There is an exact arity test for a full-support head in each of the four directed settings of Q1(a), for both coherence rules.** Let a non-point tree be $P=\bigvee_{i=1}^m\Sigma\Theta_i$, put $h_i=\dim\Theta_i$, and put $d=1+\sum_i h_i$. The following are equivalent.

1. At most one $h_i$ is at least two.
2. There is a raw term in degree $d$ on $P$ whose Gray image is the total of $P$.
3. In some dimension there is a raw head bound on $P$ whose witness has full Gray support.

When these hold, there is an **operation-case** head in degree $d$, and a full-support head in every higher degree. No smaller degree has full Gray support. When two $h_i$ are at least two, even interchange-free binary conversion cannot give a full-support term, by Response 15. For a point the vertex is the total and its iterated identities give the positive-dimensional heads. The positive construction below works for every admissible $E$ and already uses the full-support raw rules.

### A raw term for the tensor of a globe and an edge

Work in hom dimensions in this paragraph, so cut $k$ denotes global cut $k+1$. Let $x$ be the moving $r$-globe, $r\ge1$, and $b$ the stationary edge in the neighboring hom. Write $C(y)$ for the tensor of a face $y$ with $b$, in the chosen factor order. At a vertex $y$ this means the pure whisker of $b$. Write $x@v$ for the pure whisker of $x$ at a vertex of $b$. Define the initial pair of raw $r$-terms by
$$
  C(s_0x)*_0(x@t_0b),\qquad (x@s_0b)*_0 C(t_0x).
$$
Take this order for a left moving factor and the opposite order for a right moving factor. With the resulting pair denoted by $(L,R)$, perform the following updates successively for $1\le k<r$. On the left use
$$
 (L,R)\longmapsto \big(C(s_kx)*_k R,\ L *_k C(t_kx)\big).
$$
On the right use
$$
 (L,R)\longmapsto \big(L *_k C(t_kx),\ C(s_kx)*_k R\big).
$$
Here the lower $C$'s are the heads already constructed on a globe and an edge, substituted at actual faces. Every product is top composition or a whiskering by a cell of dimension $k+1$ at cut $k$.

These formulas give **literal** raw parallel faces. A fixed whisker distributes over a higher face by the recursive boundary of the binary disc paste. Thus, before step $k$, taking the $k$-pole of either expression on $x$ gives that expression on the corresponding $k$-face of $x$. The source and target of $C(y)$ on a $k$-face $y$ are precisely the previous $L(y),R(y)$. This checks both seams in either update. For $r=k+1$, the two new sources are $L(s_kx)$ and the two new targets are $R(t_kx)$. For the base $r=1$, both orders of the two edges run between the same two vertices of the square. This proves parallelism inductively, without any associativity or unit conversion.

In the Gray table the resulting pair is the signed boundary of the tensor atom. One can read the recursion directly from the tableau: appending the edge on the right changes the sign of its face contribution by $(-1)^k$ in moving degree $k$, while putting the edge first changes the sign of the moving differential. Starting with the displayed two vertex paths gives the two updates above. On this generic pair of globes the map from $F_E$ to the Gray table is an isomorphism. This is the single-edge case of the forced unary laws: after contracting the stationary edge, each proper hom cell of rank $n\ge2$ has effective moving rank at least $n-1$. A first proper inner exchange at hom cut $k$ would have to match the two distinct $k$-hemispheres of the moving globe. Requirement 4 then identifies the tables. Consequently the tensor atom really is an $E$-witness at the written faces, and the full-support head has exactly its value. This proves the recursion for $C$ in every degree and both orders. [Forced unary laws](../answered/lax-interchange-every-dimension-r8-2026-11-20.md#the-minimum-on-pasting-diagrams) · [Signed tableau recursion](../../../scripts/e6/interchange_cylinders.py) · [Steiner tensor](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0403237)

### Substitution along the outer spine

First produce a raw term for the ordinary total inside each suspended column. For two raw cells $a,b$ meeting along a literal $k$-face, use a fixed whisker or top composition when the smaller dimension is $k+1$. Otherwise compose
$$
 (s_{k+1}a *_k b),\qquad (a *_k t_{k+1}b)
$$
by this same procedure at cut $k+1$. The common $(k+1)$-face is literally $s_{k+1}a *_k t_{k+1}b$. Each recursive call raises the cut, and every head used at a fixed whisker has one argument of dimension exactly one greater than the cut. Its arity has the single moving disc with a fixed wing described in Response 3. After ordinary strict projection the resulting term is the binary composite by Godement's law. Apply this to each list of children of the column tree from the leaves upwards. Adjacent child intervals have their common vertex at that depth as a literal face, since the internal products on either interval are at higher cuts. In a single outer column the Gray hom is ordinary, so this constructs the required column value even for trees whose maximal generators have different dimensions. [Fixed whiskers and their strict values](#researcher-response-3)

Choose the tall column if present, otherwise a column of maximal height. Extend its interval to the left and right one span at a time. For a point column use the fixed whisker by its generating 1-arrow. For a height-one column use its entire local edge path as the stationary 2-term and the existing interval term as the moving input of $C$. A globe labelling uses the term and all its literal faces, so these are raw substitutions even when the moving term was itself obtained from a prism. On the Gray side the result is the tensor of the two specified cells: the enriched composition maps the universal atom by the tensor of the two labellings. Induction along the interval therefore gives the tensor of **all column totals**, including any intervening vertex factors. Its global degree is $d$. This proves (1) implies (2).

Taking the written faces of this term gives a head bound on $P$ itself. Its projected faces differ because the total contains a top tensor generator and a strongly loop-free Steiner category has no proper endomorphism. Hence this is the operation case, even if the construction of the term ended in a substitution. In higher dimensions take identities on the full term and use the full-support coherence case; its typed variables include the whole tree for the variable rule as well. Choosing one top generator in each column gives a tensor generator of degree $d-1$ in the hom, proving the lower bound. Evaluating any head at its identity tuple gives a term on $P$, so Response 15 proves (3) implies (1). In particular the four-edge cube admits a raw full-support 5-head, whereas replacing its four edges by two adjacent local 2-globes gives the inaccessible 5-arity of Response 15. [Two-column obstruction](#researcher-response-15)

There is also a prescribed-boundary consequence for the **variable** rule. On any tree satisfying (1) where $F_E(P)\to\mathfrak F(P)$ is an isomorphism, every specified strict cell is realizable at every prescribed parallel raw boundary over its faces. In each hom the Steiner category is generated by the tensor atoms. For any such atom restrict to its interval and take the generating globe of each factor. The resulting tree still satisfies (1), and substituting its raw total just constructed realizes the atom in $P$. Realize a strict paste of these atoms by the ordered compositions above. At each seam with equal Gray faces the strict $E$-faces are equal by the isomorphism hypothesis, so the variable-form identity-image transports of Response 3 fit the written faces while preserving the value. The same transport fits the final boundary. This applies in particular to a moving globe against a stationary edge in either order, with fixed exterior vertices. The arity criterion itself only tests the existence of *some* full-support head. Reflection at arbitrary old boundaries on a general computad still requires a separate argument. [Steiner atom generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.13564) · [Prescribed-boundary transport](#researcher-response-3)
