## Researcher Response 37

**For every fixed \(n\ge4\), recognizing whether the ordinary join in Q1(c) reflects all old types through degree \(n\) on a finite computad is \(\Pi^0_2\)-complete.** The same holds for full ordinary strict-image conversion. There is a computable family on which the first failing degree is exactly \(n\) or \(n+1\). Distinguishing the two computes uniform halting on finite Turing machine configurations. For \(n=4\) hardness holds on a single fixed 2-skeleton with one object, two arrows and eight generating 2-cells. Only one pair of braid repairs is declared.

### A finite presentation for configuration strings

Let \(M\) be a deterministic Turing machine on a two-way tape, with blank symbol \(\square\) and left and right moves. A configuration includes its current state and its entire tape with finite nonblank support. Whether \(M\) halts on **every** such configuration is \(\Pi^0_2\)-complete. Allowing every starting state matters here. [Endrullis, Geuvers, Simonsen and Zantema, Corollary 5.8](https://hjemmesider.diku.dk/~simonsen/papers/j11.pdf#page=17)

Use one object \(x\), arrows \(l,r:x\to x\), and the following 2-generators:
\[
 d:1_x\to l,\quad z:r\to1_x,\quad
 L_c:l\to l,\quad R_c:r\to r,\quad
 Q_{q,a}:l\to r,\quad H:l\to r .
\]
Here \(c,a\) range over the tape alphabet and \(q\) over states. A floating connected diagram is exactly a string \(dL_uQ_{q,a}R_vz\) or \(dL_uHR_vz\), with \(u,v\) finite words in their left-to-right order. Indeed each generator has at most one input and one output wire. A closed connected component must start at \(d\), change wire type exactly once, and end at \(z\). Its drawing is a tree and has no bounded faces. Thus this list also accounts for every decorated component in Response 35. Write \(T=dHz\) for the component representing the final cleaned tape.

Juxtaposition in the following rules means temporal composition of **2-cells on one wire**. For \(\delta(q,a)=(p,b,\mathrm{R})\) use the first row, and for \(\delta(q,a)=(p,b,\mathrm{L})\) use the second:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 Q_{q,a}R_c\ \to\ L_b Q_{p,c}, & Q_{q,a}z\ \to\ L_b Q_{p,\square}z,\\
 L_cQ_{q,a}\ \to\ Q_{p,c}R_b, & dQ_{q,a}\ \to\ dQ_{p,\square}R_b .
\end{array}
\]
For an undefined transition use \(Q_{q,a}\to H\). Include \(L_cH\to H\) and \(HR_c\to H\) for all \(c\). For each displayed replacement \(u\to v\), fix raw composite writings of its two strings. Declare 3-steps \(p:u\to v\) and \(j:v\to u\), then 4-cells in both directions between \(p;j\) and \(1_u\) and between \(j;p\) and \(1_v\). Thus each replacement is an isomorphism in the category of 3-cells modulo mutual raw 4-reachability. The final two declarations are \(k:e_T\to i_T\) and \(k':i_T\to e_T\), where \(e_C=\mathrm{eh}(C,C)\) and \(i_C=1_{C*_1 C}\) at a fixed raw reading of a closed string \(C\). There are no higher declarations.

On a closed string containing \(Q_{q,a}\), exactly one forward machine rule applies until \(H\) is reached. The letters of \(u\) record the cells strictly left of the head, \(a\) the scanned symbol, and \(v\) the cells strictly to its right. At an empty wing the boundary rule reads a new blank. Explicit blank padding therefore represents the same finite-support computation. After an undefined transition the two cleanup rules terminate at \(T\), in either order. Call a string good if its machine configuration eventually halts, counting every cleanup string as good. This property is invariant under each local replacement in **either** direction: for a machine step the computation from its source has the computation from its target as its tail. Every good string connects to \(T\) by a finite sequence of forward replacements.

### Transporting a repair and excluding it at a nonhalting configuration

A replacement on a closed string lifts to an invertible 3-class at the indicated raw readings. Whisker the declared steps by the parts of the string before and after the changed substring. Whiskering the two cancellation comparisons gives inverse classes, with the associativity, functoriality and unit comparisons at the composite faces. Any other grouping of the same string is reached by the invertible base paths. These statements take place in the fundamental tricategory of the raw algebra whose 3-cells are modulo mutual 4-reachability. At the identity arrow of \(x\), its 2-endomorphisms and these 3-classes form a braided monoidal category. The tensor is temporal composition and the crossing is the Eckmann–Hilton interchanger with its unit fits, represented by the raw two-variable crossing. Its naturality follows by pasting the naturality of the tricategory composition constraint and unit constraints. Equivalently evaluate that composite over the free two-scalar base, where the raw crossing and the structural crossing represent the same elementary braid with a consistent orientation, then substitute. [Tricategory on mutual-reachability classes](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-5) · [Scalar braid comparison](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-37)

If all configurations halt, choose an isomorphism \(D\cong T\) for each component. At \(T,T\) the braiding is the identity in the quotient by the two declarations. Naturality conjugates the half twist at \(D,D\) to this identity. For two different components use the composite of the two braidings, conjugated by \(D\otimes E\cong T\otimes T\). Thus both directions of every test of Response 35 have actual raw witnesses. This yields reflection through four for the join and for full strict-image conversion, at arbitrary raw boundaries in the presentation. [Ambient repair criterion](#researcher-response-35)

Suppose instead that \(C\) encodes a nonhalting configuration. We show that neither orientation at \(e_C,i_C\) can have a raw 4-cell. First quotient the ordinary strict 2-category on the wires and gates by the equations between the two string faces of each declared 3-step. In this quotient let \(I\) consist of diagrams containing a good closed component. To check well-definedness before quotienting, inspect a replacement in any planar context. It matches a segment of one wire, possibly including its original birth or death. It preserves the connectivity to the rest of that wire, also in the two boundary rules. It leaves every other component alone. If the changed component is closed, its good status is invariant by the preceding machine calculation. Planar isotopy preserves the labelled strings. These are all elementary equalities in the quotient. Further, \(I\) is closed under horizontal and temporal pasting with other diagrams: a component with neither input nor output wires survives both gluings intact.

Every ordinary strict cell of degree at least three has one 2-value in this quotient, obtained by taking its iterated 2-source or 2-target. They agree on the generating 3-steps, and agree under all compositions. A strict 4-expression using \(k\) or \(k'\) has 2-value in \(I\). At that occurrence the diagram contains two copies of \(T\). At cuts zero and one use closure under gluing. At higher cuts the two operands have the same 2-value in the quotient. At the proposed boundary on two copies of \(C\), the 2-value is outside \(I\). Hence a raw witness cannot use either repair: in the ordinary kernel a rank-four generator in the support of a raw 4-term occurs in its strict paste, by positional support and positivity. [Support in ordinary strict projection](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#support-in-the-ordinary-strict-projection)

On the remaining declarations use the permutation model, sending \(d\) to a singleton and all other 2-generators to the empty list. Both arrows go to the identity 1-cell. Every 3-step preserves the number of births and can be sent to the identity permutation, so all its inverse comparisons are interpreted by identities. Each \(C\) contains exactly one birth. Consequently \(e_C\) is the transposition of the two births and \(i_C\) the identity. The discrete higher homs exclude both raw orientations. The joined planar identity still inhabits both types. This proves the reduction to degree-four reflection, including the case where other configurations *do* halt.

### Keeping the 2-skeleton fixed

Use precisely \(d,z,L_0,L_1,R_0,R_1,Q,H\), with the same wire types as before and \(Q:l\to r\). A live closed string is \(dL_u Q R_vz\) for arbitrary bit words \(u,v\). Choose injective fixed-length binary codes \(\alpha(c)\) of length \(t\ge1\) for tape symbols and \(\beta(q,a)\) of length \(s\ge1\) for state/scanned-symbol pairs. Only the rule list depends on \(M\). The last \(s\) bits before \(Q\) specify the pair. The remaining left and right wings encode \(t\)-bit blocks, aligned starting at the head. Within each block the bits are in their usual left-to-right order on both wings.

For example, with bit words understood to use the wire type at their position, a right move has the two replacements
\[
 \beta(q,a)Q\alpha(c)\ \to\ \alpha(b)\beta(p,c)Q,\qquad
 \beta(q,a)Qz\ \to\ \alpha(b)\beta(p,\square)Qz .
\]
A left move uses \(\alpha(c)\beta(q,a)Q\to\beta(p,c)Q\alpha(b)\) and \(d\beta(q,a)Q\to d\beta(p,\square)Q\alpha(b)\). An undefined transition replaces the code and \(Q\) by \(H\). To cover *all* strings, send an invalid state code immediately to \(H\), and do the same for a short state code detected against \(d\), retaining \(d\). With a valid moving head, a full invalid block in the wing being read, or a nonempty short block at its endpoint, sends the matched head and bits to \(H\), retaining any matched endpoint. An empty wing uses the blank rule. Each condition describes a finite list of literal string replacements. Cleanup erases one adjacent bit at a time.

Every live string has exactly one successor. For a valid state code decode the wings into a finite-support tape, assigning blanks to any malformed blocks, including a partial outer block. Up to the first attempt to read a malformed block the machine steps agree with this tape. At that attempt cleanup starts immediately. Thus uniform halting implies that every coded string reaches \(dHz\), including malformed strings. Conversely every actual nonhalting configuration has a fully encoded finite spelling with its correct state and symbol. Each successor is fully encoded, extending by a blank when necessary, so this string never reaches cleanup. Eventual cleanup is invariant on every signed move by determinism before cleanup. The arguments using the ideal of good closed components and the singleton birth apply verbatim to these finite rule tables. This establishes hardness with a fixed 2-skeleton; its finite higher presentations supply the varying inputs.

### The first failing degree and suspension

For every machine in the family there is a degree-five failure at \(T\). Use the two marked repair slides of Response 6 on the written \(k:e_T\to i_T\). Their common raw boundary is from \(e_T;e_T\) to \(e_T\): whisker \(k\) on either side by \(e_T\), then apply the appropriate unit comparison. In the join the two slides both reduce to \(k\). To interpret the entire context in the marked 2-strand model of that response, again give just \(d\) a singleton. Each machine 3-step has identity permutation and its inverse comparisons may be sent to identities. Send \(k\) to the distinguished mark and \(k'\) to the absorbing arrow at its own boundary. The two slides have marks \([1,0]\) and \([0,1]\). The model has only identities between such marks. Thus the degree-five separation holds with all the machine attachments present. Degree four is the first failure in the nonuniform case and degree five in the uniform case, for both surfaces. [Marked repair and its model](#researcher-response-6)

For \(n>4\) take \(n-4\) successive ordinary bipointed suspensions. To check the lower bound on the first failure, reflection of *strict inhabitation at all old boundaries* through \(m\) implies the corresponding strict reflection through \(m+1\) on a suspension. Across the two poles, projecting the strict hom gives a strict filler at the desuspended raw faces. By hypothesis it has a raw inhabitant there. The raw restriction of the forward hom to the original computad is a weak equivalence and reflects existence at the original written faces. The equal-pole homs are equivalent to terminal homs, the reverse strict hom is empty, and the low paths lift directly. This argument never requires selecting a particular strict value for the returned witness. It applies in the uniform case with \(m=4\) and in the nonuniform case with \(m=3\), then iterates. For the matching upper bound, suspend the failing raw type and its joined derivation. The word equations suspend on the shifted cuts. Pruning and insertion suspend their spliced arities, and the disc and endo equations suspend with their boundaries. A raw filler at the suspended faces would restrict to a raw filler below. This shifts the two stated first failures exactly. [Restriction at the actual forward hom](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-11) · [Suspension of insertion](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-12)

Finally, on all finite inputs, enumerate old types and surface derivations through the specified output degree, and for each search for a raw derivation at its old type. Strict pastes and derivations of equality of their expressions are likewise enumerable for full strict-image conversion. This gives a \(\Pi^0_2\) upper bound for each fixed degree bound. Together with the family it gives completeness for every \(n\ge4\). All members of the reducing family fail unbounded conservativity. The result computes the complexity of the bounded property and does not decide recognition of unbounded conservativity for the join.

**Validation.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_configuration_braids.py CHECKOUT` with the built Rice checkout at `577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc`. The checker compares the local string rewrites against 1,568 individual tape transitions using a separate finite-support tape representation, and checks every cleanup order on 49 finite wings. On a machine with both halting configurations and a two-state cycle it checks 660 signed local moves against an explicit halting invariant. It also checks 588 binary-head windows and the malformed state codes at the fixed signature. Rice checks 34 raw judgments, including the two end moves, whiskered inverse comparisons, the actual marked slides and their first two suspensions. [Configuration and raw-type checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_configuration_braids.py)

## Researcher Response 38

**At a specified old boundary, all ten individual ordinary surfaces admit an exact restriction to the divisors of its planar grade.** In particular, degree-five reflection holds on a mixed birth-and-death presentation if no empty-input 2-generator divides that grade, or if no empty-output 2-generator divides it. There is a strongest such test among strict 2-category labellings. Recognizing when it gives the one-sided criterion is $\Pi^0_1$-complete. Even with one birth and one death of a single wire and no generating 2-scalar, some boundaries pass this test only in infinite 2-categories. These are statements about a sufficient test for reflection at an old boundary. [One-sided reflection theorem](#researcher-response-36)

### Restriction at the actual old faces

For a finite ordinary computad $X$ and $r\ge1$, let $U_r$ be the strict $r$-category freely generated through dimension $r$ with the two strict faces of every $(r+1)$-declaration imposed equal. A strict functor $F:U_r\to C$ gives a grade to every term of rank at least $r$, using the common $r$-shadow above rank $r$. Higher declarations require no further equations. In $C$ call $b$ a divisor of $p$ if a one-hole strict $r$-pasting with value $b$ in its hole has value $p$. Equivalently, start at $p$ and close under taking either factor of every well-typed composition along any cut below $r$, with ordinary identity padding. All pastes include the unit context. Let $J$ be this set, or any larger set closed under the same factor operations. Keep all declarations below $r$ and exactly the declarations of rank at least $r$ whose grade belongs to $J$. Call the resulting computad $X_J$.

Here is the support check which makes this a subcomputad and proves the restriction for converted inhabitants. In an ordinary full-support generic paste of rank at least $r$, each entry of rank at least $r$ has grade dividing the output. In a strict expression for the full cell, a composition below $r$ exhibits each operand of sufficient rank as a factor, while a composition at or above $r$ gives both operands the same $r$-shadow. Taking typed faces either preserves that grade or drops below $r$. Identity padding carries the old grade through. Induction over the expression and its typed support proves the assertion for all supplied entries. Equivalently on a pasting tree truncated at $r$, all the parallel versions of an $r$-slot in a higher string have equal grades by the incidence condition, and the $r$-slots are composed by the lower cuts. A head of rank below $r$ has arity of lower rank. Thus each required written attachment restricts, and a raw term of retained grade strengthens by recursively using its very same head and the strengthened tuple. Weakening the result returns the literal original term.

For surface conversion one can use the family argument of Response 32 with a single restriction at a retained grade. Prescribe the restricted faces first. At a head of retained grade the support argument supplies all entries of the supporting tuple, so apply the same head at those entries in $X_J$. At other grades there is no restriction to supply. Successive restrictions use the same subtuple as restriction after substitution. For a word equation on a larger arity first apply the word support retraction, which fixes both writings and their types. Each used grade is then a divisor on either side. For pruning, insertion, disc and endo removal the restricted or joined tuple is obtained by the same substitutions as before weakening. Hence the equations hold on these restrictions, including at converted faces. This gives an inverse to weakening on inhabitants and their surface classes of retained grade. The argument works also when combining the ordinary surface equations. For an old query of degree $r+1$ its two faces must have the same grade for a witness to exist; for higher queries use their already common shadow. [Restriction on proper word supports](#researcher-response-5) · [Families at converted faces and the additive case](#researcher-response-32)

Take $r=2$ at a degree-five query. If either set of exceptional generators is disjoint from the divisors, the restricted computad satisfies Response 36 and its raw witness weakens to the original faces. This permits arbitrary higher declarations at the retained grades. For $F=1_{U_2}$ a 2-generator divides the query grade exactly when **some planar diagram representing that grade uses it**. A factorization in the quotient can be lifted by choosing expressions for its other factors: the quotient by the 3-relations leaves the graph paths and composability of 2-cells at lower cuts unchanged. Conversely cut out the indicated occurrence in any such expression, serializing its planar diagram if needed. Equivalence of representatives is the congruence generated by replacing the planar poles of 3-generators in either direction. Thus a degree-five counterexample requires an undirected chain from its 2-shadow to a diagram using a birth and one to a diagram using a death. The endpoints of these chains may differ. Functors preserve factorizations, so no other strict 2-category test can exclude a generator admitted by the universal one. A finite table test closes the set of divisors until stable. This can be used again on the indexed hom of Response 32 before its shift to degree six.

### How much a planar grade can distinguish

Use objects $s,t$, three parallel edges $i,m,o:s\to t$ and a loop $h:s\to s$. Take one birth $B:1_s\Rightarrow h$ and one death $D:h\Rightarrow1_s$. At $m$ put the letters of any finitely presented monoid $M$ as 2-endomorphisms, imposing its word relations by 3-generators. Also take $l:i\Rightarrow m$, $d:m\Rightarrow o$. For words $u,v$ let $p=lud$ and $z=lvd$, in vertical execution order. One more 3-declaration imposes
$$
                    z=(B\,D)*_0 z .
$$
At literal weak faces use $1_{1_s} *_0 z$ on the left, with the same parenthesization of $z$ on both sides. The two terms are then parallel before any conversion. The grade $p$ is available at an old degree-five type, for example by adjoining a 3-endomorphism of $p$, three parallel 4-endomorphisms of it and two consecutive 5-steps. Other declarations above dimension three do not change $U_2$.

**The universal one-sided test passes at $p$ precisely when $[u]\ne[v]$ in $M$.** Equality exhibits both factors by the displayed relation. For inequality the following explicit 2-category separates them. It works for any monoid image $N$ distinguishing the two words, including $N=M$. Take the hom from $s$ to $s$ to have only the unit 1-cell, with 2-endomorphism monoid $\{1,e\}$ where $e^2=e$. The hom at $t$ is terminal and the backwards hom empty. In the hom from $s$ to $t$ use $i,m,o$ in that order. Each strictly forward arrow carries an element of $N$, as does each endomorphism of $m$; at $i,o$ use only the unit monoid label on endomorphisms. All these arrows carry an additional bit. Compose by multiplication and Boolean OR. Identify the two bits just at the arrow $i\to o$ labelled $[v]$. This is a congruence: its vertical neighbours at the two outer ends have unit monoid label. Left action by $e$ sets the bit and right action at $t$ is trivial. Setting the bit is composition with a central idempotent endomorphism at each object, giving the strict horizontal action and interchange. Send $h$ to the unit, $B,D$ to $e$, the letters to their unmarked labels, and $l,d$ to unmarked unit labels. The relation holds by construction. The ideal generated by $e$ consists of $e$, the marked arrows in the forward hom, and the identified arrow at $[v]$: it is closed under either-sided horizontal and vertical composition, and each such forward arrow is obtained by action of $e$. In particular the unmarked arrow at $[u]\ne[v]$ excludes both exceptional factors.

All nonnegative additive weights on the 2-generators that respect the 3-relations give $B,D$ weight zero, by the same displayed relation. At rank one $h$ has unit value in every category labelling of Response 32 and both declarations are placeable at the initial vertex of the query. Thus these earlier tests cannot give this exclusion. With $M=\langle P,Q\mid PQ=1\rangle$, $u=QP$, $v=1$, the normal forms $Q^aP^b$ give distinct values. In *any finite strict 2-category*, the images of the two letters are elements of the finite vertical endomorphism monoid of the image of $m$. A one-sided inverse there is a two-sided inverse. Hence the relation exhibits both exceptional factors in every finite probe, while the infinite probe gives degree-five reflection at the query.

In general failure of the universal one-sided test is recursively enumerable: enumerate planar factorizations and finite congruence derivations until both exceptional kinds have appeared. The monoid family gives hardness of its complement by the many-one simulation of the halting problem in the finite Thue word problem. Finite-probe success is recursively enumerable by enumerating finite strict tables and assignments. It is undecidable already on group inputs to this same family: a separating finite group gives the displayed finite 2-category, and a finite 2-category separating the factors must distinguish the words in the finite image group at $m$. An algorithm could test all generators against the unit to decide whether a finitely presented group has a nontrivial finite quotient. [Post's Thue simulation](https://doi.org/10.2307/2267170) · [Bridson–Wilton, Theorem A](https://arxiv.org/abs/1401.2273)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_planar_divisors.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/PlanarDivisorChecks.agda` with an existing directory. The solver builds the strict tables for all six absorbed labels of $S_3$, checks both composition laws and interchange, then saturates planar divisors at all 36 choices of absorbed and queried label. On a literal degree-five composite it checks all 36 assignments of the two letters, of which 30 exclude both exceptional gates. It strengthens the context, term and type and checks that weakening gives back the exact originals. The bicyclic calculation also checks the absorbing relation and $PQ=1$ on their written faces and strengthens the composite at $QP$ using the complement of the marked ideal. Agda 2.8.0 checks both restricted degree-five terms and the original attachments. [Planar divisor solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_planar_divisors.py)

## Researcher Response 39

**At an old boundary, the directed 3-histories between its two planar poles suffice to restrict the entire computad, even for witnesses of arbitrarily high dimension.** Thus through degree five it suffices that the planar diagrams on those histories collectively omit every 2-generator with empty input, or collectively omit every 2-generator with empty output. This test can certify mixed presentations on which **every** planar grade in Response 38 admits both exceptional factors. An effective finite version uses a strict 2-category with a compatible preorder on parallel 2-cells. The exact directed test sometimes requires an infinite such category.

### A lower-dimensional slice through a specified occurrence

Let $A,B$ be the strict 2-source and 2-target of the query, of degree at least three. For degree three these are its two displayed faces. Use the free strict 2-category on the 2-skeleton, retaining the directions of the 3-declarations as contextual transitions. Consider the planar states $P$ for which there are directed strict histories $A\Rightarrow^*P\Rightarrow^* B$. Let $H$ be the union of the sets of 2-generators occurring in all these states. Keep the entire graph, the 2-generators in $H$, and all higher declarations whose attaching dependency closure uses only such 2-generators. When the boundary has a strict history this restriction gives exactly the same raw inhabitants and the same ordinary surface classes at the written type in every degree. For a degree-three boundary with no such history even strict inhabitation is impossible. An old type of higher degree already includes an old 3-cell at those planar poles.

To prove the assertion, take **any chosen 2-generator occurrence in the closed support** of a strict cell $c$ of dimension at least three. There is a strict 3-cell at the two iterated 2-poles of $c$ whose closed support contains that generator. At dimension three use $c$ itself. At a generating cell of higher dimension the occurrence is in an attaching face, so descend into that face, which has the same planar poles. For an identity descend to its base, padding to degree three when necessary. In a composite at a cut below three take the required 3-cell from the operand supplying the occurrence and the iterated 3-source of the other operand, using identities for lower-dimensional factors. These fit at the actual cut; their paste still contains the occurrence by the union law for closed ordinary strict support. At a cut at least three, both operands already have the exterior planar poles, so retain the selected operand's slice. This induction on strict expressions and attaching dependencies uses a potentially different slice for each generator.

Serialize the resulting strict 3-cell as a directed sequence of single 3-steps in stationary planar contexts. At each step the two written attaching diagrams occur in its input and output states; the remaining stationary factors are already present there. Thus every 2-generator in its closed support occurs on this history at an actual state. This is the directed support calculation of round-1 Response 124 at rank three, now applied to a slice of a cell of *arbitrary* higher rank. It puts the complete 2-support of any proposed strict filler, and of the old faces, inside $H$. The pullback of the raw and ordinary surface inclusions over the free strict inclusion then gives the stated equality of inhabitants, including the original converted typings and classes. It applies to the individual surfaces and to their ordinary join. A superset of $H$ gives the same restriction statement. Applying Response 36 on the restricted computad gives degree-five reflection for each individual surface under the displayed one-sided condition. [Closed-support pullback](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-27) · [Serialization of a strict paste](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#which-attachments-can-alter-an-old-type-in-either-kernel) · [One-sided reflection](#researcher-response-36)

For a certificate, interpret the 2-skeleton in a strict 2-category $C$ with a preorder on parallel 2-cells monotone under both pastes, and require $F(\partial^-g)\le F(\partial^+g)$ for each 3-generator. A 2-generator in $H$ must have grade dividing some $p$ with $FA\le p\le FB$. In a finite table one may generate just the necessary preorder from the 3-declarations by stationary pasting, reflexivity and transitivity. Compute the interval, then close under planar factors. The free 2-category with the actual contextual reachability preorder makes this test exact: a factor expression for a state is a diagram with the indicated occurrence and strict planar equality preserves generator support. The interval criterion also applies in a crossing indexed hom before shifting by one degree in Response 32.

### A family separating directed from undirected tests

Use the graph, monoid letters and 2-generators of Response 38. Write $b=B D$ for the bubble, $p=lud$ and $z=lvd$. Give every word relation at $m$ a 3-declaration in **each** direction. Give the presentation an unconditional emission $1_{1_s}\Rightarrow b$ and a single guarded consumption $b *_0 z\Rightarrow 1_{1_s} *_0 z$. Use the query with both planar poles $p$. For instance the consecutive 5-steps over a generating 3-endomorphism of $p$ from Response 38 give a literal old type there. That extra 3-endomorphism preserves the planar state. Additional declarations above rank three are unrestricted.

Every undirected planar test admits $B,D$ as factors: emission identifies $b$ with the unit at $s$, so $b *_0 p=p$ in every 2-category grading by equations. In contrast, a directed returning history at $p$ uses either exceptional gate **if and only if** $[u]=[v]$ in the presented monoid. For equality, perform the word derivation, emit, consume at $z$ and undo the derivation. For necessity one can count births: the history starts and ends with zero and only consumption can decrease the count. Erase $h,B,D$ to identities and read the central monoid label throughout. At consumption $lvd$ has to occur in the erased diagram $lud$. The delimiters are the only arrows out of $i$ and into $o$ other than identities in this erased presentation, so equality of the central labels is necessary. Equivalently use the directed interpretation below. The exact one-sided test here is $\Pi^0_1$-complete by the same Thue simulation as in Response 38, even conditional on failure of every undirected 2-category test. The upper bound enumerates two finite directed returns, or in general two paths from the source to the target, exhibiting the two exceptional kinds.

For a concrete ordered interpretation use the category of marked monoid arrows from Response 38 but keep the two bits distinct even at the label $[v]$. In each hom compare only equal monoid labels, with $0\le1$. At $i\to o$ of value $[v]$ allow also $1\le0$. This is compatible with the two pastes since only unit labels can act vertically beyond these delimiters. At the sole 1-cell at $s$ put $1\le e$ and let $e$ mark an arrow. The emission and consumption are ordered as required. If $[u]\ne[v]$, the interval from the unmarked query value back to itself contains only that value. Its planar divisors exclude $e$, the value of both $B,D$. A finite monoid detecting the word difference gives a finite certificate.

There are instances missed by **all finite preordered 2-category certificates**, as well. Take $PQ=1$ in both directions, $u=QP$ and $v=1$. The bicyclic monoid distinguishes these words and gives the infinite certificate. In any finite target quotient each parallel 2-hom by mutual reachability. This respects both pastes by monotonicity. In the finite vertical endomorphism monoid at the image of $m$, $PQ=1$ now holds, hence $QP=1$. Before the quotient this gives mutual reachability between the query and guarded values. Travel to the guard, emit the bubble there, consume it and return. The intermediate value contains both exceptional factors, so the original finite preorder cannot exclude them. This explains why even the correctly oriented finite algorithm has an intrinsic limit. [Finite table separation and complexity source](#researcher-response-38)

**Reproduce.** In an existing directory `DIR` run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_directed_planar_divisors.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/DirectedPlanarDivisors.agda`. The solver generates the contextual preorder from precisely two image steps and compares directed and symmetric intervals on all 36 letter assignments in $S_3$. Both gates divide a middle state in all 36 symmetric intervals, and neither does in 30 directed intervals. The solver projects and literally refolds the degree-five composites and checks the bicyclic restriction from 22 to 18 declarations. Agda 2.8.0 checks the restricted composites and an actual raw returning 3-history that emits and consumes the bubble at the guarded boundary. ## Researcher Response 40

**Each of the ten individual ordinary surfaces is conservative in every dimension on every finite iterated planar substitution of Response 16. Bubbles may occur in every block, including the root and nonterminal blocks.** More generally, for either insertion or the full binary surface with disc removal, substitution into the 2-generators of an arbitrary finite planar computad preserves the existence of a surface model raw weakly equivalent to the free realization, provided each inserted presentation has such a model for that surface. Consequently the two-degree result for arbitrary higher extensions applies above each of these bases. For the *join*, the first-failure classification on the same iterated presentations is instead that of Response 16. [Planar substitutions and their attaching poles](#researcher-response-16) · [Replacement under higher attachments](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-32)

### The outer planar model with its actual gate transports

Fix an ordinary 2-computad $P$. Use a Gray-category $G(P)$ whose objects and 1-arrows are the vertices and edge words. Its 2-cells are **serial** planar histories of generating gates at actual offsets. Between two histories its 3-cells are paths of planar switches modulo endpoint homotopy in the planar tier complex. A switch specifies the actual ordered simultaneous row, even if another row gives the same serial ends. The presentation on paths is by the disjoint-switch squares and actual three-gate hexagons. Indeed with individual gate marks each tier cell is a product of permutohedra. The two-skeleton gives those relations. On forgetting the marks, each tier still orders every event by row and then by position in its row, so its stabilizer on vertex marks is trivial. Equivalently, lift paths and relations with the marks fixed at their start. This accounts for paths which induce a nonidentity permutation at the same unmarked serial end. [Marked and unmarked planar tier spaces](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#the-planar-diagram-spaces-are-aspherical)

At fixed object poles the hom 2-category uses vertical concatenation of histories and the corresponding whiskering and pasting of switch paths. The two orders of whiskering paths agree by disjoint-switch squares. Spatial whiskering shifts each positioned step onto the indicated substring. For cubical composition use the order left block then right block. Its compositor moves one block across the other by the rectangle of actual separated switches, using the horizontal order specified by the two input substrings. Different sweeps agree by disjoint-switch squares. Splitting a block subdivides the rectangle, and moving a switch from one block past a gate in the other gives the hexagon for the two simultaneous gates of that switch and the separated gate. With three side-by-side factors the elementary crossings are the same whiskered switches in either bracketing and the three-block reorderings agree by the same hexagon. Empty histories give the units. These are the strict hom and associative cubical composition laws of a Gray-category. At two empty substrings the left-right order specifies *which* crossing, whose inverse traverses that edge backwards. There is no identification of the two different horizontal orders at a shared empty gap.

For the published surface take $K(G(P))$, and for the binary surface take the history model $E(G(P))$ of round-1 Responses 5, 11 and 26. Denote the selected model by $Q(P)$. The raw generator map from $H_P$ is a weak equivalence. For $K$, at fixed 1-paths all raw components of the space of 2-cells are 1-types by the marked planar theorem. Serialization supplies the vertices and the two-row comparison maps to the class of its specified switch: its two column triangles are the identity on the chosen order and the cubical compositor for the two rows, with orientation given by the order. Rows before and after them whisker the same switch. This is the two-row calculation of Response 10 one dimension lower. Thus the map induces the equivalence of fundamental groupoids, including the loops coming from permutations. Its target fixed hom is exactly that groupoid. Below this comparison the graph flags are the free paths; number of gates rules out equivalences between distinct paths and the zero-gate component at an identity has no braid. For $E$ take the comparison on singleton atoms through the raw span with $K$. Normal evaluation identifies its 3-arrows at any two history objects with the paths between their normal evaluations, conjugating at the two ends. It is essentially surjective on 2-objects by singleton atoms. These arguments give the comparison for each palette of copies of the gates as well. [Evaluation of a row exchange](#reflection-at-the-written-boundary) · [History atoms, normal forms and the binary equations](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#interchange-at-the-binary-positions)

In $K$ the positions of a 2-cell are the actual generating events of its serial word. In $E$ use the generating events in the **flattened planar word of its normal evaluation**. An atom itself may contain many such events or none. Each 3-arrow transports these positions by its actual planar path. Giving fresh tags at the start lifts that arrow uniquely to the tagged models at the correspondingly tagged end. At a binary 2-object the normal representative sorts using binary addresses and stationary paths, which are unchanged by tagging. Thus assigning tags there tags each constituent atom history uniquely, and exchanges transport the tags by the rectangle of constituent switches. Applying the sorting comparisons gives exactly the two endpoint transports on arrows. For $K$ tagging commutes with concatenation, spatial shifts and the chosen cubical comparisons. Hence forgetting tags in either construction commutes with the surface operations, including specialized heads. This is the extra structure needed from the planar base; an arbitrary unstructured choice of a weak equivalence to a surface model would not by itself specify these transports.

### Raw substitution over a moving diagram

For each gate $g$ let $K_g$ be the inserted presentation, with a point for a stationary gate, and write $\mathcal D_g$ for its free realization. Over two fixed outer paths the inner category of the substituted computad has the following description. For each underlying labelled planar diagram type $w$, take its fully vertex-marked tier space $T_w$ with the set $O(w)$ of identified gate occurrences. Form
$$
 \left(T_w\ \times\!\prod_{o\in O(w)}\mathcal D_{g(o)}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}(w)} .
$$
Take the disjoint union over $w$. The action transports the marks and the corresponding product factors together. One may include every component of the space with the indicated marks. The formula is an inner higher category: at an inner nerve rectangle $R$ use the space $\mathcal D_{g(o)}(R)$ in each factor. In particular, the base at that rectangle includes the full marked tier space, even when its gate permutation is trivial.

For discrete colour sets at the gates this is the marked mesh calculation itself. A fully marked lower mesh takes one independent colour at each of its events. A refinement transports its labels identically on the marks. Forgetting the marks is the action groupoid of simultaneous relabellings of that coloured mesh. The formula holds with outer cuts as well, since the external wire ports are ordered and gluing transports the tags of each piece to those of the pasted diagram. For space-valued colours resolve by simplicial sets: at a fixed inner rectangle the horizontal globe attachments are the parallel coloured gate attachments of the planar substitution calculation. Horizontal localization commutes with this geometric realization, and realization in spaces commutes with finite products. It commutes with the homotopy orbits as both are colimits. This gives the displayed formula from the discrete one, naturally in the rectangle. For inner Segal and completeness use the projection to the uncoloured planar groupoid. Locally over it the displayed spaces are products of inner nerves, with transition equivalences permuting the identified factors. The Segal pullbacks use fibre products over this same groupoid (equivalently pull back along the marking cover and check fibrewise), so the tests hold there and descend. The outer identity has no gates and the old graph flags are unchanged. This supplies the fixed-hom calculation at the written lower poles using the usual graph boundary fits. [Space-valued colour resolution](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#a-product-formula-for-substituting-higher-presentations-at-top-generators) · [Planar mesh gluing with automorphisms](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#the-planar-diagram-spaces-are-aspherical)

### Surface operations and comparison at the fibres

Choose a raw weak equivalence $H_{K_g}\to UY_g$ to the selected surface base of each child, using cofibrancy and fibrancy as in Response 17. Decorate $Q(P)$ as follows. Its lower objects and paths stay as they are. A 2-object carries an object of $Y_g$ per actual gate position. A 3-arrow carries the underlying planar 3-arrow and a child arrow for each input position, ending at the label of the **transported** output position. In degree $2+j$, $j>1$, the planar boundaries have the same 3-arrow and there is a child $j$-cell on each input position at its specified two child faces. Thus pure braids remain in the base, while two paths with different transported label endpoints cannot be compared by forgetting their positions.

Here are the head operations. Tag the contributing planar positions distinctly, transporting their names along the 3-strings of the arity by its planar arrows. A higher slot forces equality of the transports on its two 3-faces. Evaluate the head in the tagged $Q(P)$. In rank two the chosen planar paste uses the events of each contributing 2-slot once, including inside binary atoms. Above rank two the generic source visits exactly one 2-position from each transport class on the supporting tree. Hence a particular output tag can be read in the weight-one cut model $W_2(Y_g)$ of round-1 Response 37: give its position its child label wherever it occurs in the input, and use the weight-zero value on slots where it is absent. Use the child's track on the corresponding input strand at higher entries. Evaluate the same head there and take its weight-one result. Its two faces use precisely the labels at the indicated transported ends. Below degree two use the base operations.

For a nested evaluation the same tag splits back to the same actual input occurrences. On planar histories this follows by flattening each substituted word; in the binary model the intermediate sorting exchanges expand to their rectangles, with the endpoint comparisons composing. At the level of the generic tree this is the distinguished-position splitting, with at most one occurrence of the tag in each relevant 2-source. Thus the weight-one readings after substitution agree with those from the original pieces. In a surface equation the two evaluations in the **tagged** planar model agree, so the same tag and child tuple are selected on the common support. The $W_2(Y_g)$ equation supplies the equality for each selected coordinate. Use the retracted support for a word equation and the joined arity for insertion. Disc removal holds in the chosen full binary model. This is the tagged strand construction of round-1 Response 37 with actual planar gate positions in place of scalar positions. It does not require a gate to be at a crossing object cut in the ambient computad. [Weight-one cut and tagged surface operations](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-37) · [Two arity cuts for private attachments](#the-outer-diagrams-and-the-local-coordinates)

Assign the generators at each replaced gate to singleton positions with their indicated child values. Its private suspended attaching terms evaluate there by the two arity cuts, which recover the actual child operations. This gives the raw comparison to the decorated surface model at the original weak attaching paths. To verify it over fixed outer paths, forget the child entries. The base comparison is the equivalence to the planar groupoid proved above. Over a marked base diagram the target is the product with the child models: a twice-suspended hom head cuts back to its original inner head on each named position. On the source the corresponding fibre is the product of the $\mathcal D_g$ by the mesh calculation. At a serial representative the fibre comparison is the product of the chosen child equivalences. Indeed the fibre is obtained by putting one inner label at each gate of this fixed mesh. On its private suspension the comparison is the chosen generator map. Pasting the labelled mesh on the target selects that very input for each tag, with the other slots stationary. This holds for the whole inner rectangle and its restrictions by the cut-substitution formula. Equivalently one can check on a serial paste of the single-gate rectangles; the outer flag fits and choices of interleaving for that paste are contractible. This identifies the map on fibres without choosing a trivialization along a moving pure braid.

Finally the source is the indicated locally constant family over its planar groupoid and the target has unique tag transport along each planar path, acting by reindexing the factors. Over marked representatives the preceding fibre equivalences therefore give a fixed-hom equivalence after descent. More explicitly, a mapping category at untagged ends splits by the bijection from the chosen start marks to the end positions. For each such bijection use the mapping category at those tagged ends, **retaining all planar paths with that bijection**. Every target 2-object admits start marks, and pasting tagged inputs before forgetting them gives the same comparison. Together with the graph flags this proves the asserted raw weak equivalence. Induction from the arbitrary planar leaf models now proves the all-dimensional result. By round-1 Responses 31–32, above such a generating $r$-skeleton all arbitrary higher attachments preserve reflection through $r+2$ ($r\ge2$). The substitution hypothesis still requires that each child's attaching terms live at its one outer gate.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_braided_planar_substitution.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/BraidedPlanarSubstitution.agda` with an existing directory. A birth creates the two edges of a lens, a death consumes them and an internal point lies between them. Starting with two closed lenses, the solver enumerates 19 serial words and 38 distinctly tagged words. The possible return permutations are exactly the identity and the simultaneous exchange of both triples of gates. Exchanging the lenses twice returns every tag but has signed birth crossing two; the signed count is invariant by the planar squares and hexagons (forget all points except the two birth events in a planar braid). Independently quotienting the actual two-colour schedules gives 36 components, with eight fixed colourings and 28 paired colourings at the chosen word. The solver also checks 360 transports of local updates and reads both orders of two different child letters at either inner gate. Agda 2.8.0 checks the mixed terms on the two closed lenses with their padded weak path poles and the selected child terms.
